# LANE IN6 — measured result and unresolved ship gate

No commit, staging, push, deployment or external network request. The lane's expected UTC/ratchet-only finish condition is **not attained**: search currency and non-UTC source defects remain. This report records the result, not a submission-ready claim. The UTC precision decision and integrator's ratchet signature remain untouched.

HEAD: `60c5cd67074beff328a745952ab31a17c0195ba7`. Required base: `60c5cd67074beff328a745952ab31a17c0195ba7`.

## Measurement boundaries and hardcode disclosure

| Static input/policy | Dynamic output | Boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Existing FACT/HM contracts and held source files | Exact spans, hashes, per-field validation | No retrieval instant fabricated; FACT and gate unchanged |
| Existing trial sets and statistical engine | Rebuilt review objects and source-keyed RoB lookup | No synthesis, membership or search implementation edits |
| Named renderer fields and interpretation alternatives | Registered visible units and planted-error tests | No blanket HTML registration or new scanner exemptions |
| Existing sealed search measurement | Current engine hash and raw-file inventory | Old search result not relabelled as newly measured |
| Fixed replay snapshot date already held by project | Cache replay and byte comparisons | Build date is not source retrieval evidence |

MEASURED means tool output from this checkout. INFERRED marks a diagnosis supported by source inspection. CLAIMED legacy audit commentary is rendered as an OWED JUDGEMENT, not promoted to source-verified FACT. Registration coverage is not evidence validity.

## Source fixes and integration repairs

1. **FREEDOM-CVO GI harm.** Pre-fix exact refusal:

```text
L1: HARMS_INCOMPLETE -- Gastrointestinal adverse events: HARMS_INCOMPLETE -- 1 known reported outcome(s) unresolved (FREEDOM-CVO) among 9 source-reporting trial(s); extracted k=1. The page must not render this as harm absence.
```

The held FDA EMDAC text, section 3.3.2.1/Table 14, reports recurrent symptom events/rates per 100 person-years, not the unique-patient composite GI risk numerator. A canonical `EFFECT_PRESENT_ESTIMAND_CLASS_MISMATCH` refusal now contains the exact held span and document digest. No symptoms were summed and no recurrent-event rate was converted to a risk. Numeric examples in the source are event counts, not patient counts. The original extract is retained below. Tests validate the real held span and reject a fabricated span.

2. **Canonical HM dispatch and numeric verification.** Pre-fix: `source differs from document-bound span`, `UNLOCATED_VERIFIED_SOURCE`, and HM3 `assert not ... harms_incomplete` / `assert row['verified'] == 'verified'`. Canonical HM inputs use `source_span`; they had been routed through CGX's different `span` contract. The source-specific dispatcher now validates HM's held-span contract, retains `source_span` and `outcome` through all override/fallback copy paths, and still runs numeric verification. FACT separately refuses missing UTC. A planted wrong count is rejected. Existing harm-source numeric inputs are preserved; the generic any-adverse-events signal now recognises the generic registered wording.

3. **PCSK9 held-source binding.** A rebuild exposed `pcsk9-mace/25773378: cited document digest mismatch`. The existing AACT source had been converted to held JSON, while the extracted-count row retained the old pipe-text hash/source. Rechecked NCT01507831, EG001 alirocumab vs EG000 placebo, and all four counts/denominators against the held JSON; only the source binding/hash was corrected. The corresponding regression rejects a changed count. This is a source-format repair, not a new clinical estimate.

4. **RoB sensitivity.** Pre-fix esketamine membership-consistency assertion failed after display trial keys became family keys. `rob_sensitivity.levels` now uses the canonical source trial key when reading the source-keyed RoB table. Primary membership and numeric values remain tested. The membership engine is unchanged.

5. **Endpoint typing.** Pre-fix finerenone compatibility was `ASSERTED_HETEROGENEOUS_UNDERLYING_HOMOGENEOUS`, expected `CONSISTENT`. A selected secondary outcome no longer inherits an unrelated primary endpoint's components merely because the same abstract names a primary endpoint. The fallback requires the selected source itself to identify a primary endpoint. A secondary-effect fixture tests this restriction.

6. **Publication renderer and statistical-layer integration.** The candidate already contained IN4's late legacy-strand identifier fallback, sealed scorecard gate entries and suppression of invalid GRADE aggregate statistics. IN6 retained these source fixes and rebuilt their consumers. It did not edit the leak scanner, scorecard checker or publication gate. The final complete standard below measures execution and PASS/REFUSED rather than assuming inherited repairs passed. Family rows, envelope/fragility/decomposer fields and summaries are now typed, recomputable projections of their input records. Count summaries enumerate records; no topic result constants were added.

7. **Remaining prose.** Outcome summaries support reported effects and count/continuous/event-time input schemas while retaining FACT refusal on their dependencies. Missing/invalid pool states are recorded judgements, not invented estimates. Legacy definition audits, eligibility records, protocol controls/divergences, model adjudications, parity snapshots, non-pooling assessments, retrieval gaps and scope amendments now render from named structured input branches. Their commentary is explicitly adjudication-owed. Overstated historical wording such as every number being verified was removed. Exact retraction statements and scope-mismatch wording were restored in typed renderers and checked against the required base.

8. **Held legacy provenance.** Where an existing document identity and exact source span can be located in held committed bytes, added paths/hashes/span offsets. JSON abstract tokens retain their actual escaped source bytes. Missing retrieval instants were not inferred from `2026-09-11`. Rows lacking a demonstrable binding remain UNVERIFIED_FACT; independent all-field counts below distinguish those from UTC-only rows. This does not cure derived-number or other historical source defects.

9. **Census and manifest currency.** Manuscript FACT coverage counts now derive from all outcome input rows, matching the graph coverage denominator. Error-rate inventory refresh labels new rows `NOT_INDEPENDENTLY_RECHECKED`; it does not manufacture a new blind audit. Canonical override inventory tracks current source rows and retains an explicit independent-adjudication boundary. Rebuild/replay and manifests are verified below.

## Test requirement versus obsolete pinned behaviour

| Test area | Resolution |
|---|---|
| HM3 source harms / IN3 span plants | Harness fix: canonical source contract dispatch and metadata preservation; malformed spans and numeric plants still refuse |
| HM3 unchanged primary membership | Test compares actual selection fields and numeric values; additive provenance/display fields no longer masquerade as membership changes |
| Legacy limitation comparison | Test checks the current limitation object registry, states, acknowledgements and retraction rendering instead of obsolete one-banner/one-object HTML assumptions |
| Membership/parity/RoB | Source-keyed RoB fix; parity test inspects explicit family counts; risk predicate test reads typed state spans while retaining its old-renderer controls |
| Typed effect display | Full outcome fragment must include source typing tables; the significance-only fragment deliberately excludes source excerpts |
| Legacy untyped gate plant | Plants missing type identifiers explicitly; the live corpus is no longer assumed untyped |
| GRADE positive control | Clears unassessed domains when testing a fully assessed fixture; display test expects human-readable publication-bias label |
| PM merge/retrieval | Updated typed interpretation counts and enumeration boundary object; unbound prose plants still refuse |
| Harm registration | Requires current source-backed per-item states, rather than requiring historical extraction debt to remain unresolved |
| GLP1 FACT graph/UI | Not weakened: missing-UTC FACT failures remain failures |

## Bounded follow-up after the first complete standard

The first complete IN6 standard measured 1062 passed / 22 failed unit tests and 7 PASS / 4 REFUSED limbs. Its transcript is retained below. The second pass addresses the measured integration failures:

* Family cells now hash only the displayed fields and family identity. The original compact-versus-full identical-table test is retained.
* Subgroup evidence-unit summaries and recorded estimand decisions are restored as typed projections; a changed-subgroup plant verifies the summary changes. Scope amendments retain their structural heading.
* Missing held snapshots leave provenance unbound instead of crashing the core builder. The negative fixture confirms FACT still refuses.
* Existing legacy strand members receive derived type metadata only after their PMID (and NCT when present) matches the held record. Member identities, numerics and selection remain unchanged. Existing target binding axes/coercions are used, with unknown axes disclosed. No type verdict is a FACT verification. An unheld-identity plant and a binding-axis plant remain refused.
* Cross-source, protocol-control, zero-claim, never-considered, k=2, effect-scale, reconciliation and count-chain tests inspect the current typed objects and complete fragments instead of obsolete character windows or literals. The hand-edited HTML test now asserts its visible plant actually changes the page. The HM fixture explicitly binds its own held document.
* Windows HTTP tests use exclusive ownership of 127.0.0.1:8000, retry a busy port for a bounded interval, and compare HTTP bytes with their intended local file. Other listeners are never killed.

These repairs do not relax the FACT, gate, membership, search or visible-prose scanner contracts.

## FACT retrieval decision — MEASURED

Denominator: canonical `outcomes[*].trials` input rows, counted once per outcome, across the 32 review objects. Rendering duplicates and strand-only views are not counted as new rows. Every non-UTC FACT field is evaluated independently without substituting a fabricated timestamp, so a first missing-UTC verdict is not assumed to be the only defect.

```text
{
  "N_rows": 147,
  "N_pages": 32,
  "date_only_rows": 90,
  "date_only_pages": 28,
  "other_reason_rows": 50,
  "other_reason_pages": 20,
  "first_verdicts": {
    "missing retrieved_utc in UTC": 96,
    "missing full document_sha256": 44,
    "PASS": 7
  }
}
```

## Search refusal — MEASURED boundary, INFERRED remedy

The old measured engine hash differs from the current engine. All named raw snapshot directories and local archive paths are absent from this checkout. Earlier local-mirror searches did not locate a complete replacement snapshot set. {
  "outcome": "Gastrointestinal adverse events",
  "kind": "typed_refusal",
  "provenance": "EFFECT_PRESENT_ESTIMAND_CLASS_MISMATCH",
  "source_level": 2,
  "document_ref": "outputs/handover/glp1_regulatory/fda_media_172242_ITCA650.pdf.txt",
  "document_sha256": "e27b9985959e139b9c84f15b7e364d3c82a2fed861819e2b777f6b3f5aef963e",
  "source_span": "Typical AE analysis methods measure incidence (i.e., number of patients reporting at least one AE, or time to \nfirst event), rather than counting all occurrences (i.e., frequency of event). Frequency of events may also be \nrelevant since individual patients—especially those most susceptible—may have recurrent events (but are \ncounted only once for incidence). Therefore, CDER assessed cumulative number of events as well as incident \nevents of nausea, vomiting, diarrhea. Figure 10 shows that there are increases in GI events (including all events, \nnot just the incident event) related to the initiation of treatment (with the 20 mcg/day device) and titration \n(transition to the 60 mcg/day device). In addition, there are increases in events related to subsequent device \nremoval and insertion procedures (the “staircase” pattern with 6-month intervals), as well as accumulation of \nevents beyond periods of recent device changes. Finally, CDER identified only three SAEs of nausea, vomiting, \nand diarrhea among subjects who received ITCA 650, versus none among subjects who received placebo; \nhowever, review of narratives for other SAEs identified cases in which GI symptoms were reported in the \n\n### PAGE 44\n44 \ncontext of these other events but not coded as GI SAEs in the datasets. Taken together, this suggests that the \nrisk of nausea and vomiting is not restricted to the period of treatment initiation. \nTable 14. Treatment-Emergent AEs of Nausea and Vomiting, and Diarrhea (On-Treatment Narrow FMQ*) in FREEDOM \n \nITCA 60 mcg/day (n=2070) \nN (Events per 100 PY) \nPlacebo (n=2074) \nN (Events per 100 PY) \nRate Difference \n(Events per 100 PY) Rate Ratio \nAll nausea 564 (25) 86 (3) 22.0 7.8 \nMild \nModerate \nSevere \n330 (14) \n197 (7.9) \n37 (1.4) \n59 (2.2) \n24 (0.9) \n3(0.1) \n13.4 \n8.1 \n1.8 \n6.4 \n8.9 \n12.9 \nAll vomiting 392 (16) 26 (1) 15.2 16.9 \nMild \nModerate \nSevere \n238 (9.5) \n125 (4.9) \n29 (1.1) \n17 (0.6) \n7 (0.3) \n2 (0.1) \n10.2 \n5.5 \n1.4 \n15.2 \n19.1 \n15.1 \nAll diarrhea 198 (8) 87 (3) 4.7 2.4 \nMild \nModerate \nSevere \n128 (5.1) \n65 (2.5) \n5 (0.2) \n56 (2.1) \n30 (1.1) \n1 (0) \n4 \n2.1 \n0.3 \n2.4 \n2.3 \n5.2 \nSource: CDER Review staff. Analysis: R v. 4.3; data: adae.xpt and adsl.xpt from SDN0000. \n* FDA MedDRA Query (FMQ) Version 2.1. FDA has developed MedDRA queries (FMQs) based on grouping related PTs to search the clinical safety database \nfor safety signals (see also Section 3.3.2.2 and Appendix 5.2 for a complete list of PTs in these queries). \nInvestigator attribution of intensity. \nAbbreviations: AE, adverse event; FMQ, FDA Medical Query; ITCA 650, exenatide in DUROS device; MedDRA, Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities; \nPT, preferred term; PY, person-years",
  "reason": "FDA EMDAC section 3.3.2.1 and Table 14 report recurrent symptom-event counts and rates per 100 person-years for nausea, vomiting and diarrhea. These are not arm-specific unique-patient counts for the registered composite gastrointestinal adverse events RR. Summing symptom counts would double-count patients and treating event rates as risks would change the estimand. The held text does not supply the required composite patient count; no composite count is reconstructed."
}
